#2913. Dycom (DY) Q3 2027 Earnings Call Transcript

Long-haul builds lift the outlook as fiber pacing cools.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 24, 2026 at 4:08PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Dycom (DY -18.02%) reported third quarter results that exceeded the high end of its guidance across all primary financial metrics, including revenue, adjusted EBITDA, and adjusted EPS. Management raised the company's full-year revenue outlook to a range of $7.66 billion to $7.73 billion, supported by a record total backlog of $12.9 billion. The company stated that significant growth in long-haul and data center interconnect projects is expected to serve as the leading growth driver for the Communications segment in fiscal 2028, helping to offset a projected decline in fiber-to-the-home revenue. Additionally, the company announced a new $200 million share repurchase authorization following the completion of $100.5 million in buybacks during the third quarter.

Operator: Good day, and welcome to the Dycom Industries, Inc. Third Quarter 2027 Results Conference Call [Operator Instructions] Please be advised that today's conference is being recorded. I would now like to hand the conference over to Ms. Callie A. Tomasso, Dycom's Vice President of Investor Relations and Corporate Communications. Please go ahead.

Callie A. Tomasso: Thank you, operator, and good morning, everyone. Welcome to Dycom's fiscal 2027 Third Quarter Results Conference Call. Joining me today are Dan Peyovich, our President and Chief Executive Officer; and Drew DeFerrari, our Chief Financial Officer. Earlier this morning, we released our fiscal 2027, third quarter results along with certain outlook information. The press release and accompanying materials are available in the Investor Relations section of our website, including the outlook expectation summary document, which provides additional outlook metrics beyond what will be discussed on today's call. These materials, which we will discuss during today's call include forward-looking statements made pursuant to the safe harbor provisions of the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995.

Our discussion and these statements reflect our expectations, assumptions and beliefs regarding future events and are subject to risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially. A detailed discussion of these risks and uncertainties is included in our filings with the SEC. Forward-looking statements are made as of today's date, and we undertake no obligation to update that. Additionally, we will reference certain non-GAAP financial measures during today's call. Explanations of these measures and reconciliations to the most directly comparable GAAP measures can be found in our press release and accompanying materials. With that, I will turn the call over to Dan Peyovich.

Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: Thank you, Callie, and good morning, everyone. We appreciate you joining us today. We delivered another strong quarter with record revenue of $2.06 billion and adjusted EBITDA of $311.7 million or 15.2% of revenue. Adjusted EPS was $5.23, every one of these results exceeded the high end of our guidance. Our performance this quarter is a direct result of our market leadership in digital and critical infrastructure paired with strict discipline in project selection to maintain the quality of our revenue and backlog. Quarterly revenue grew 41.6% year-over-year, including 11.2% organic growth in communications. Consolidated adjusted EBITDA grew 42% and adjusted EPS grew 33% year-over-year, demonstrating our commitment and ability to deliver attractive returns for shareholders as our platform scales.

During the quarter, we significantly accelerated our share repurchases, taking advantage of the compelling value in Dycom shares to buy back $100 million of our common stock. As announced this morning, our Board of Directors has replaced the prior program with a new $200 million program for future share repurchases. We finished the quarter with record total backlog of $12.9 billion, a book-to-bill of 1.3x and year-over-year backlog growth of 57%, including 36% growth in communications. This growth gives us visibility and confidence in our long-term growth prospects and we continue to diversify our backlog across demand drivers, positioning ourselves for durable growth in the coming years. Turning now to our segments, beginning with communications.

We had a strong third quarter and expect a solid finish to the year. Communications revenue grew more than 11% in the quarter and is up 17% year-to-date. That performance is backed by robust underlying demand. The country's ever-increasing data consumption requires significant sustained investment in digital infrastructure, specifically in the outside plant and inside plant networks we've built. Our foundational service and maintenance platform directly supports our high-growth program bills, maintaining and repairing our customers' networks day in and day out embeds us deeply into their operations giving us unmatched visibility into their infrastructure, establish local workforce capacity and trusted relationships at the field level.

That presence, combined with our national scale and deep technical expertise gives us a decisive advantage in winning and mobilizing large-scale deployments across numerous types of build programs, including fiber-to-the-home, BEAD, long-haul and data center interconnect, copper reclamation and wireless network modernization and densification. We are pursuing large, long-term opportunities across the diverse programs that is driving durable growth in communications. Following this year of incredible growth, we expect next year to return to a more normalized growth cadence. Based on our current visibility into customer build plans, we anticipate mid-single-digit revenue growth in the Communications segment for fiscal 2028, accelerating to higher growth levels in fiscal 2029.

We also expect adjusted EBITDA margins to expand year-over-year, and we will provide formal fiscal 2028 guidance on our fourth quarter call. As we have continued to diversify our business, our fundamental growth drivers remain strong, with some near-term shifts in customer dynamics as pacing in some programs moderates and others accelerate. Let me walk through each of those drivers. I'll start with fiber-to-the-home, which has been our largest demand driver this year, fueled by increased deployment across key customer programs and our strong competitive position, which has resulted in a significant increase in our leading share of the market. This has been a year of exceptionally strong growth in our fiber-to-the-home business with revenue up 50% through Q3.

We continue to see many years of activity ahead as customers continue to reiterate their long-term build commitments. Having said that, the macro environment, combined with a higher cost of capital, is having an impact on regional fiber overbuilders and some programs have slowed. What's most relevant to Dycom is the effect this is having on several of our large customers who, as the market has seen, have in recent weeks indicated decisions to moderate the pace of their fiber-to-the-home builds next year. Given this near-term shift in dynamics, we expect fiber-to-the-home revenue to be down in fiscal 2028 when compared to fiscal 2027, though still performing at levels above fiscal 2026.

It's important to note that our customers remain committed to their build-out plans. So we expect this to ultimately result in a higher pace in future periods or an extension of the fiber-to-the-home build cycle which increases our confidence in the sustainability of that growth over the longer term. Next is BEAD, many of our customers are preparing for rural fiber passings through the program. We remain well positioned to capitalize on these opportunities and continue to expect construction to start in earnest next year. Continued rapid growth in data consumption, cloud compute and AI are driving the need to connect data centers across the country.

We have spoken in the past about the multiple long-haul and data center interconnect opportunities we have been pursuing as customers broaden their fiber investments, and we continue to make strong progress on this front. Long-haul and data center interconnect builds are tracking ahead of initial expectations and will be the leading growth driver for our Communications segment next year. We got out in front of this demand early, performing several hundred millions of dollars of work in place over the past 2 years.

From there, we are well positioned for future network modernization and densification opportunities. Lastly, customer consolidation is providing another meaningful tailwind. We continue to expect further consolidation across network operators in the coming years. As our customers scale and integrate acquired footprint, they consolidate their vendor base around national partners with the balance sheet, safety record and workforce capacity to execute across multiple states, a trend that favors Dycom. We have been deliberate in diversifying our backlog to balance the timing and velocity of each demand driver and taken together, the program builds provide sustainable growth opportunities well into the next decade.

Our early identification and pursuit across the diverse set of programs is driving the continued growth of the segment as these new programs more than offset the moderating base of fiber-to-the-home work. Our record backlog, deep customer relationships and position at the center of both converged broadband and AI infrastructure give us tremendous conviction from strong growth in the years ahead. Shifting to Building Systems. Our strong execution continued this quarter, driven by robust demand for both electrical and structured cabling systems. We delivered solid growth in the quarter while also securing new awards that extend our visibility beyond calendar 2028.

In addition, the integration of National Technology Integrators is progressing well, and the business is performing in line with our expectations. Our strategy is clear: differentiate through performance and pursue markets with resolute drivers for growth. Evidence of our success can be seen in our revenue growth and margin performance in the DMV region. It's also visible in our growing structured cabling platform, which has expanded in 19 states as key customers bring us into new geographies to support their build-outs. As we engage with our customers to partner on their builds, we have not observed material changes in underlying customer behavior or plan scope as a result of recent policy and permitting uncertainty.

In fact, customer conversations continue to reinforce our confidence in the scale and duration of the data center build cycle. Looking ahead to fiscal 2028, we expect Building Systems to generate total revenue growth in the high teens or better with adjusted EBITDA margins that range from the high teens to low 20s, building on a year of exceptionally strong performance in fiscal 2027 and because this higher-margin segment is growing faster than communications, we expect it to drive continued consolidated margin expansion. Demand continues to grow, our focus remains steadfast, and we are well positioned to capitalize on the long runway ahead.

Reflecting our outperformance in Q3, we are raising our full year revenue outlook to range from $7.66 billion to $7.73 billion. This represents an increase of approximately $125 million at the midpoint compared to our prior guidance range. Moving to our strategic priorities, I'd like to highlight 4 updates. First, we are pleased with our ongoing progress in talent and workforce development, remaining an employer of choice and training a best-in-class field workforce for central to our ability to scale across complex programs. Second, expanding our Building Systems segment and further diversifying the enterprise remain key priorities. The addition of our Building Systems segment has been accretive on every metric, increasing consolidated margins and improving cash flow.

And while we continue to grow our footprint organically, we are looking selectively at M&A in strong markets. Third, margin performance remained solid, with consolidated adjusted EBITDA margin increasing to 15.2% and reinforcing the meaningful earnings uplift we're capturing as we extend our platform and demonstrating our ability to drive compelling shareholder returns as we scale. Fourth, we remain focused on operating cash flow discipline and expect seasonal benefits in Q4. This quarter, operating cash flow was temporarily impacted as we work with customers who are consolidating businesses. Our DSOs were 112 days, and we expect this to normalize and revert to more recent levels in the coming months.

In closing, we are executing our strategy, and our results and record backlog demonstrate both our leadership and digital infrastructure, and our ability to deliver certainty on complex, large-scale program. Significant awards and further diversification in our communications program builds combined with the success of our Building Systems segment, position us well for long-term profitable growth. Our people are the core of who we are and our success is a direct result of their work every day across America. I want to thank our more than 20,000 teammates for delivering safely and at the highest level of quality. With that, I'll turn the call over to Drew for further details on the quarter and our outlook.

H. Andrew (Drew) DeFerrari: Thanks, Dan, and good morning, everyone. We had a strong quarter, driving double-digit growth in revenue, adjusted EBITDA and adjusted EPS. We also continue to invest in our future growth and repurchased $100 million of our shares during the quarter. Q3 total contract revenues of $2.06 billion grew 41.6% over Q3 of last year. This reflects the strength of our relationships and continued diversification across our customer base. Consolidated adjusted EBITDA of $311.7 million or 15.2% as a percentage of total revenue increased 42% over Q3 '26.

And consolidated adjusted net income was $158.1 million and adjusted diluted EPS of $5.23 per share increased 33% over Q3 '26 and Communications revenue of $1.615 billion grew 11.2%, driven by robust fiber-to-the-home programs increased long-haul and data center interconnect builds and continued maintenance and operation services. Adjusted EBITDA for communications of $206.4 million was 12.8% of segment revenue, reflecting continued investments to scale our operations and approximately 50 basis points of cost pressure in the segment from higher fuel prices. During Q3, a rural customer filed for Chapter 11 bankruptcy protection and another smaller customer experienced significant financial difficulties.

As a result, the company reserved for uncollectible accounts receivable totaling $22.7 million or $17.1 million net of taxes or $0.57 per diluted share. The majority of the balances was for work performed last year. There was no revenue from these customers in Q3, and we have no backlog of work for either of these customers. Moving on to Building Systems. Revenue was $441.7 million as we continue to experience significant growth in this segment. Adjusted EBITDA was $105.2 million or 23.8% of segment revenue as the business performed exceptionally well. Total backlog at the end of Q3 was $12.9 billion, including $11.2 billion of communications backlog and $1.7 billion of Building Systems backlog.

Backlog expected to be completed in the next 12 months is $6.7 billion, including $5.4 billion from communications and $1.3 billion from Building Systems. Strong cash flows remain a key focus area. The combined DSOs of accounts receivable and contract assets net were 112 days, reflecting the timing of work and collections during the quarter and the temporary effects from customer consolidation. We expect DSOs to revert to normalized level in the coming months. As we look ahead, we expect normal seasonal cash flow trends, and we expect to generate several hundred million dollars of operating cash flow during Q4. During Q3, we prioritized share repurchases and bought back 345,000 shares of our common stock for $100.5 million.

This week, our Board of Directors approved a new $200 million authorization for share repurchases through May 2028. This authorization replaces the remaining amount of our prior authorization and further enables us to continue to opportunistically repurchase shares. We ended the quarter with cash and equivalents of $202.8 million, total liquidity of $949 million and pro forma net leverage of approximately 2.3x adjusted EBITDA, providing us with financial flexibility for continued strategic growth and investment. Based on our outperformance in Q3, we have raised our full year revenue outlook, and we now expect total contract revenues to range from $7.66 billion to $7.73 billion.

This revised outlook is an increase of approximately $125 million at the midpoint compared to our prior range of expectations. For the Communications segment, we now expect full year contract revenues ranging from $6 billion to $6.04 billion for Building Systems. We now expect full year contract revenues ranging from $1.66 billion to $1.69 billion. We are maintaining our fiscal '27 outlook for consolidated adjusted EBITDA margin, that is expected to increase compared to last year, comprised of strong margins in the Building Systems segment, partially offset by a decline in Communications margins from cost pressures to scale the operations and from higher fuel costs.

For the fourth quarter, we expect consolidated revenues of $1.63 billion to $1.70 billion, adjusted EBITDA of $204 million to $218 million and adjusted diluted EPS of $2.48 to $2.78 per share, excluding the impact of intangible amortization. Our outlook for Q4 reflects normal seasonal factors such as fewer available workdays due to the holidays, reduced daylight work hours and winter weather conditions. As a reminder, Q4 of last year included 14 weeks of operations based on our fiscal calendar compared to only 13 weeks in Q4 of this year.

Our performance this quarter highlights Dycom's position as a leader in digital infrastructure and our backlog and line of sight to customer programs with confidence in our continued growth in the years ahead. Operator, this concludes our prepared remarks. You may now open the call for questions.

You've heard multiple of our customers have talked about it. They've had success and getting the ability now to start decommissioning those, one, through regulatory approvals. This is a different skill set. This is a different type of equipment. We have our own proprietary piece of equipment that we're using.

Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: Increasing the baseline typically, Frank.

Operator: Our next question will come from Adam Thalhimer from Thompson Davis.

Adam Thalhimer: Can you quantify the long-haul fiber awards in November? And then also, can you give us some color what you're seeing on the bidding side for those projects?

Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: Yes, roughly, very roughly half and half, half within the quarter and half in between from an award perspective. Yes. From a competitive set, there's -- when you have $20 billion of opportunity out there, it continues to be a very active space, no different to the competition we've seen across the different demand drivers. But as I said earlier, we really think this is where we're well positioned. One, we're really first on the ground, we've been doing a ton of this work. Two, we're getting ahead from a training perspective.

We're getting ahead from an equipment perspective because of the breadth of opportunities that we're engaged in and what we see for that growth and what we see for that backlog. So we believe we're very well positioned, Adam, but it's going to continue to be a competitive space.

Adam Thalhimer: And then, Dan, how should we think about the margin profile for that work versus fiber-to-the-home?

Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: Overall, I would put them in a similar range. The difference that I mentioned earlier is on the fiber-to-the-home if you're rapidly expanding or quite frankly, if a particular market is a particular market, particular geography is slowing down, you're going to have a different kind of margin pressure, but you're going to get a lot more consistency on the long-haul and data center network.

Adam Thalhimer: And then on the Power Solutions side, have you looked at or are you looking at any opportunities outside of the DMV?

Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: You can see significant growth. They're tracking for something around 40% growth year-over-year. That's a lot when you're starting at over $1 billion of revenue, and it started with almost 3,000 electricians and growing. So significant growth in their space today. That is something that we're always looking at. And I would point to our structured cabling, where now we're in 19 states, so a ton of growth there and ability to really flex and move to where our customers need us. So we're looking at all of that collectively within the Building System, I think we're well positioned to continue.

Operator: Our next question will come from Richard Choe from JPMorgan.

Richard Choe: Just wanted to follow up a little bit on the long-haul and the copper reclamation. I think this gives you some better visibility longer term. And then same with long-haul, maybe just like the pacing of how it should kind of contribute to revenue? And then I have a follow-up.

Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: Yes. long-haul, it's a little earlier for us than we talked about going back 2 years, Richard, just because we've done really well on getting that work. That's going to go all the way as long as the fiber-to-the-home builds go, that's when you can start pulling out the copper. So you still got 5 or 6 years at least, and it's going to trail beyond that. So lots of opportunity to continue to pull out copper. And as everybody can imagine, with decades and decades and decades of putting copper in the ground, there's quite a bit to take out nationwide.

Richard Choe: And with there being a little bit of a shift here with the, I guess, near-term fiber plans, maybe it seems like you're saying the long-term plans still seem to be in place for a fiber-to-the-home? Does it make sense to adjust maybe your cost structure a little bit to be some kind of pullback here or some shifting? Or do you think that's too kind of shortsighted.

Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: So we don't look at it. Our crews in the Communications segment are highly fungible. So we've been training folks to move into the long-haul into the data center interconnect work, we've been working on that for a while. That's the investments that you saw this year were part of that. So with growth, we're going to have head count growth. That's part of where we're going next year. So we're really looking at just reallocating those resources.

Operator: Our next question will come from Steven Fisher from UBS.

Steven Fisher: Just wanted to follow up on the fiber-to-the-home. I'm really trying to understand what are the biggest factors that are causing this decline next year? Is it consumer health? Is it cost of capital? Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: Our customers are reaffirming their overall build programs, Steven. Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: Yes. Very, very, very small amounts that would be in our backlog today. So we're still looking to get that converted -- that number has certainly grown over time. Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: Sure. Again, this quarter, you saw share repurchases and you saw an increase in the authorization. Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: Yes. Exactly right. Exactly right. There's -- as they consolidate, there's things that as Drew noted this quarter, there's things you have to work through. But overall, it's a huge benefit for us, and we expect that to continue.

So often the biggest challenge is not -- there are challenges there. So every help certainly is wanted and will definitely help to build. Daniel (Dan) S. Peyovich: It's a great point. So we are seeing fungibility between what we're doing structured cabling, so inside the data centers. And then what we can do on our communications side of the business. So folks that are trained to splice fiber, whether it's indoors or outdoors. We are seeing opportunities to flex across those groups, which is going to help with our future growth opportunity and just goes to, again, the investments that we've made in training, the investments that we've made in our people to ensure that we can continue to grow across the enterprise.

#2908. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2907. Anthropic IPO: Here's What a $5,000 Investment Could Look Like in 5 Years

Anthropic's $81.3 billion IPO came with eye-popping revenue growth, but roughly $895 billion in compute commitments and a swing back to an operating loss in Q3 are hard to ignore.

By Johnny Rice – Nov 24, 2026 at 4:17PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Anthropic ›

One of the most anticipated initial public offerings (IPOs) in market history has finally gone public. Anthropic (ANT -6.54%), the company behind Claude, priced its offering at $83, above the initial target. Those shares began trading on Nov. 19, opening at $111.50 before finishing their debut session at $97.17, still 17% above the IPO price.

Dario Amodei. Image source: Getty Images.

The company raised a whopping $81.3 billion, making it the largest public debut ever, surpassing even Space Exploration Technologies' (SPCX -5.65%) mega-IPO earlier this year. And at Thursday's close, Anthropic was worth $1.76 trillion on a fully diluted basis, instantly making it one of the largest public companies on the planet.

If you manage to get ahold of $5,000 in Anthropic stock, what might that investment look like five years from now?

Anthropic brought in $41 million in revenue in 2023 and $4.6 billion in 2025, then did $33.2 billion in just the first nine months of 2026, up roughly 1,200% from the same stretch a year earlier. That is incredible growth that we really haven't seen at this scale.

The bull case centers around this continuing. The bet is that Claude gets embedded in business processes across industries, and the sort of wholesale transformation of work we've seen in coding happens elsewhere.

One of the most encouraging figures for the bull thesis is Anthropic's net revenue retention, which sits at about 900%. That means customers find Claude valuable enough not only to stick around, but to scale their usage -- and spending -- ninefold in a single year.

The bear case is really centered around what all that growth costs. Anthropic's amended filing showed about $895 billion in purchase commitments and leases, mostly for compute and infrastructure -- the chips and data centers its models run on. About half of that is due before the end of 2030. And while Anthropic notched an operating gain in Q2 this year, it swung back to a $567 million operating loss in Q3.

And then, of course, the bear case also assumes the incredible revenue growth we've seen slows down considerably. In the bear case, Claude remains highly valuable in the programming world, but its broader use across most of the rest of the economy is limited.

The truth is that it is remarkably hard to predict where a stock like Anthropic's will be even a year from now. This truly is terra incognita.

That said, I believe it will be closest to the bear case. I'm not sure investors appreciate just how precarious Anthropic's position is. It must commit to spending on compute and infrastructure well before it actually books the corresponding revenue. And with usage-based billing, its customers' spending habits can turn on a dime.

My biggest issue is that I fail to see a durable moat. I think the most likely scenario is that artificial intelligence (AI) will look more like a commodity in a few years, and that Anthropic will find that its customers are happy to look elsewhere if it means they can pay less.

#2901. Why Analog Devices Stock Fell Nearly 20% Today

The chipmaker crushed fourth-quarter estimates, so why did Wall Street hit the panic button? Let's look under the hood at the guidance quirks and spending plans behind today's sell-off.

By Anders Bylund – Nov 24, 2026 at 4:26PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Analog Devices ›

Analog Devices (ADI -17.37%) reported fourth-quarter results early Tuesday morning. The analog and mixed-signal chip designer crushed Wall Street's consensus estimates and followed up with in-line guidance.

And investors hated it. The stock fell as much as 19.7% in the morning session, recovering slightly to a 16.6% drop as of 3:23 p.m. ET.

Analog Devices' headline figures were great in Q4 2026. Earnings rose 80% year-over-year to $4.06 per share. Revenues jumped 42% to $4.37 billion. The analyst consensus had called for earnings near $3.89 per share on roughly $4.32 billion in top-line sales. The guidance ranges for next quarter were broadly in line with current Street projections.

But the drop makes sense when you dig below the usual numbers. Q1 revenue guidance is in line with Street expectations, but it's still a disappointing slowdown after a fantastic Q4 result. Operating margins will fall due to high component costs and holiday-season factory shutdowns.

Moreover, Analog Devices' chip-making facilities are running at full capacity, limiting the company's ability to actually take on more orders. More manufacturing capacity is on the way with a 150% year-over-year increase in capital expenditures, but the new production lines won't make any chips until the second half of 2028.

Analog Devices is a victim of its own roaring success. When your factories are already humming at full tilt and channel inventories are finally back to normal levels, there simply isn't much room left for upside surprises in the near term.

Instead of another beat-and-raise quarter, shareholders are getting a massive $1.6 billion to $1.9 billion capital spending bill next year -- and they won't see a single chip roll off those new lines until fiscal year 2028. The stock entered this report priced for perfection, trading at a beefy 37 times trailing earnings after Tuesday's big drop. From there, in-line guidance and a long wait for the next growth spurt were all the excuse traders needed to hit the eject button.

#2900. Crypto Winter Persists, 24/7 Trading Arrives, Kalshi Under Pressure

"Bloomberg Crypto" covers the people, transactions, and technology shaping the world of decentralized finance. Today's guests: Bullish President Stacey Cunningham, OKXice Co-Chair Andrew Cuomo, Bain Capital ventures Partner Matt Harris, NYU Faculty & Monarq Asset Management Chief Operating Officer Professor Ayesha Kiani, and Vanderbilt Law School Associate Dean & Professor of Law Yesha Yadav. #2899. Lazio, mercato di gennaio: i conti decidono tra via libera e blocco

La Commissione esaminerà i dati al 30 settembre; a Formello si teme uno stop, ma resta la possibilità di intervenire sui conti.

La Lazio attende l’esame dei conti al 30 settembre per sapere come potrà operare nel mercato di gennaio: liberamente, a saldo zero o con un blocco. Secondo quanto riportato da Calciomercato.it, A Formello prevale un leggero pessimismo e il club si aspetta lo stop, che potrebbe però essere superato con le misure previste dall’articolo 90 delle Noif.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La Commissione che ha sostituito la Covisoc valuterà la documentazione presentata dalla società. Se la Lazio avrà raggiunto il parametro dello 0,70%, potrà muoversi liberamente. Se la situazione sarà peggiorata rispetto a marzo, il mercato sarà bloccato; negli altri casi, il club opererà a saldo zero.

La Lazio arriva da una sessione estiva a saldo zero. Al 31 marzo il rapporto tra costi e ricavi aveva superato la soglia dello 0,70%, raggiungendo lo 0,82%, con un disavanzo di circa 20 milioni di euro e un conseguente blocco parziale.

Nel recente bilancio, il costo del personale è salito da 98,2 a 104,6 milioni di euro e gli ammortamenti dei calciatori da 32,7 a 38,4 milioni. Il monte ingaggi è invece sceso da 79,5 a circa 75 milioni. Il club ha inoltre inserito a bilancio 7 milioni legati a Polymarket, limitati alla quota del primo anno dell’accordo quinquennale di sponsorizzazione, bloccato dall’autorità.

Incideranno anche gli incassi del botteghino e i mancati introiti europei. La scorsa stagione la Lazio aveva messo a bilancio circa 20 milioni dagli ingressi allo stadio; in quella attuale, lo sciopero del tifo ha bloccato questa entrata. In caso di stop al mercato, la società potrebbe pagare una sanzione e coprire l’eventuale deficit per rientrare nei parametri. Lotito sta cercando di sistemare il passivo inserendo crediti o dilazionando alcuni pagamenti, in particolare quelli relativi al periodo Covid.

#2898. Stock Market Today, Nov. 24: Nvidia Gives Back $240 Billion in Market Value Despite Wall Street Upgrade Signaling 29% Upside

After staging its biggest rally in more than two decades, Nvidia is surrendering some of those gains as renewed selling pressure casts doubt on the sustainability of the AI recovery.

By RJ Fulton – Nov 24, 2026 at 4:29PM EST

Nvidia (NVDA -5.86%) fell 5.86% on Tuesday, November 24, closing at $155.38 as a historic semiconductor rally lost momentum. The decline followed Monday's extraordinary 31.48% surge, Nvidia's largest single-day percentage gain in more than two decades, as investors welcomed Jensen Huang's reinstatement as CEO.

Tuesday's reversal erased a portion of those gains as renewed selling pressure swept across the semiconductor industry. Trading volume reached 379.2 million shares, approximately 107% above its three-month daily average of 183 million, reflecting continued volatility and heightened investor activity.

The S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.50%) fell 0.50% to 7,201, and the Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -1.48%) dropped 1.48% to 23,734. Semiconductor design and accelerated-computing peers also weakened, with Advanced Micro Devices (AMD -8.86%) closing at $413.27, down 8.86%, and Broadcom (AVGO -3.98%) ending at $301.08, down 3.98%.

Nvidia's sharp reversal following Monday's historic rally highlights the volatility surrounding AI stocks as investors reassess their valuations and long-term growth prospects. However, the recent price swings contrast with the company's underlying financial performance. Nvidia reported fiscal third-quarter revenue of $115.8 billion, representing 103% year-over-year growth, and raised its fourth-quarter outlook, suggesting demand for AI infrastructure remains exceptionally strong.

Wall Street also remains relatively optimistic about Nvidia's prospects. Bernstein recently upgraded the stock to Outperform while maintaining a $200 price target, implying approximately 29% upside from Tuesday's closing price of $155.38. Although analyst targets are far from guarantees, the upgrade suggests some investors may view the recent sell-off as an attractive buying opportunity rather than evidence of deteriorating fundamentals.

Looking ahead, Nvidia's ability to sustain its growth will ultimately matter more than short-term market volatility. Investors should pay particular attention to whether major technology companies maintain their AI infrastructure spending, alongside developments involving U.S.-China semiconductor restrictions and regulatory scrutiny.

#2893. Abercrombie (ANF) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Tariff refunds lifted margins as Abercrombie's sales climbed through the holiday push.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 24, 2026 at 4:38PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Abercrombie (ANF +19.69%) reported third quarter results reflecting its 16th consecutive quarter of sales growth, with management citing balanced performance across both brand families and all three geographic regions. The company stated that it is executing on strategic priorities including brand expansion, gross margin stabilization, and technological investment. Management reported that strong cash flow enabled continued share repurchases and reinvestment in the store fleet, with the company remaining on track for its fourth consecutive year of net sales growth.

Operator: Good day, and thank you for standing by. Welcome to the Abercrombie & Fitch Third Quarter Fiscal Year 2026 Earnings Call. [Operator Instructions] Please be advised that today's conference is being recorded. I would now like to hand the conference over to your first speaker, Mo Gupta, VP of Investor Relations. Please go ahead.

Mohit (Mo) Gupta: Thank you. Good morning, and welcome to our Third Quarter 2026 Earnings Call. Joining me today on the call are Fran Horowitz, Chief Executive Officer; Scott Lipesky, Chief Operating Officer; and Robert J. Ball, Chief Financial Officer. Earlier this morning, we issued our third quarter earnings release, which is available on our website at corporate.abercrombie.com under the Investors section. Also available on our website is an investor presentation. Please keep in mind that we will make certain forward-looking statements on the call.

These statements are subject to the safe harbor provisions of the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995 and are subject to risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially from the expectations and assumptions we mentioned today. These factors and uncertainties are discussed in our reports and filings with the Securities and Exchange Commission. In addition, we'll be referring to certain non-GAAP financial measures during the call. Additional details and reconciliations of GAAP to adjusted non-GAAP financial measures are included in the release and the investor presentation issued earlier this morning. With that, I will turn the call over to Fran.

Fran Horowitz: Thanks, Mo, and thanks, everyone, for joining. I'm pleased to report another strong quarter for our business. Net sales grew 11% to a third quarter record of $1.4 billion, ahead of our expectations and our 16th consecutive quarter of growth. We've grown the business each quarter for 4 years running, and that momentum continues. You've heard me emphasize the importance of balance, and we showed it again this quarter. All 3 regions and both brands delivered double-digit net sales growth with each seeing growth rate accelerate from second quarter levels. The strong top line, coupled with disciplined inventory management, enabled us to deliver healthy sales growth with higher AUR and lower promotions.

This performance flowed to the bottom line, where we delivered an operating margin of 15.5%, nicely above our expectations. And we continue to execute against our capital allocation priorities, investing in marketing, stores, digital and technology, all to support long-term growth while increasing net income per diluted share 62% year-over-year to $3.82. We also used our balance sheet to repurchase $100 million of shares in the quarter, bringing our year-to-date total to $382 million or 9% of shares outstanding at the beginning of the year. This marked our 11th straight quarter of share repurchases. Our teams continue to deliver for customers around the world, and we entered the important holiday season with momentum across brands and regions.

With that strong performance, we are raising our full year net sales and earnings expectations. We continue to expect fiscal 2026 to mark our fourth consecutive full year of net sales growth with consistent strong double-digit operating margins and significant cash return to shareholders through share repurchases. Turning back to the quarter. All 3 regions delivered double-digit net sales growth. The Americas grew 11% with stable traffic trends as we moved from back-to-school into fall. EMEA delivered 12% net sales growth with comparable sales up 4%, a significant sequential improvement from the second quarter. Germany performance improved nicely as the team continued to dial in marketing, promotions and inventory to customer traffic and demand.

Several other European markets returned to growth on healthy conversion trends, and the U.K. continues to be a consistent growth market. APAC performed ahead of our expectations again this quarter, growing 24% on strong traffic, particularly in stores. While still evaluating the right go-to-market strategies in the APAC region, the team has continued to invest in marketing and manage inventory, and we've seen stronger top line performance, especially in Hollister. Diving further into the brands, Hollister had a great back-to-school season to start the quarter and carried that momentum throughout, delivering 11% net sales growth on top of 16% growth and record third quarter sales last year.

As we discussed last quarter, the team prioritized receipts heading into back-to-school to support customer demand, and we continue to Read & React as the season progressed. That discipline allowed us to keep inventory tight, reduce promotional levels and deliver higher AUR. Growth was balanced across men's and women's with fleece, knits, graphics and bottoms performing well. Hollister's denim campaign with rising star Freya Skye drove strong social engagement and positive fan response to events in both the U.S. and U.K., demonstrating the power of connecting with our customers with culturally relevant talent and experiences.

Following a strong third quarter, Hollister is bringing an exciting flow of newness throughout the holiday season, including new product drops at Target as our continued partnership gives us another way to reach new and existing customers. In our owned and operated stores, we've invested to ensure our customers have the latest brand experience. Year-to-date, we've opened 23 new stores and remodeled or rightsized 48 Hollister stores, a substantial portion of our fleet. Overall, Hollister has made considerable progress, and we're excited for what the brand can do to cap off 2026. At Abercrombie brands, we had an excellent transition into fall with net sales growing 12% and accelerating nicely from the second quarter.

Inventory remained tightly managed and strong product acceptance allowed us to deliver improved AUR and lower-than-expected promotions. Growth was balanced across men's and women's and across tops and bottoms. Women's knits and sweaters performed particularly well, while men's saw strength across jeans and pants. Our active assortment under Your Personal Best continues to perform well. We recently showcased YPB through a partnership with Barry's, the fast-growing global fitness brand, bringing a collection of studio-ready activewear to its highly engaged community through Barry's studios and our direct channels. Abercrombie's momentum extends to our store fleet, an integral part of how customers experience the brand.

Our A&F SoHo store has continued to exceed expectations since opening in July with YPB, footwear and accessories all part of the experience. We've already brought elements of that modern A&F expression to 15 stores in major markets just in time for holiday. We're midway through another successful season with the NFL, connecting with fans in more ways across product, partnerships and experiences. As an official fashion partner of the NFL, the Dallas Cowboys and Dallas Cowboys Cheerleaders, we're engaging fans at game time events and offering our NFL assortment at select stadiums and on nflshop.com. And in time for holiday, we recently launched a collection co-designed with Cowboys wide receiver CeeDee Lamb.

The NFL assortment also extends to abercrombie kids with styles for boys and girls. As we move into the holiday season, we're offering a new digital gift card on the Abercrombie app and website, featuring ideas for every age from babies and toddlers to big kids and adults. Creating memorable holiday experiences has been part of Abercrombie for more than 130 years, and we're building on that heritage in new and relevant ways as we look to finish the year strong. In addition to our strong quarterly results, we've made meaningful progress against our 4 priorities for the year.

That includes growing our brands and owned and operated business while adding new avenues of growth, stabilizing gross margins against external cost pressures, investing in tools and technologies like AI to improve speed and efficiency and maintaining strong profitability to fuel excess cash return to shareholders through share repurchases. On that first priority, our growth continues to start with the customer, a consistent focus that has resulted in net sales growth every quarter for 4 consecutive years. Our teams have stayed close throughout the year, testing, learning and evolving our assortments alongside engaging marketing across brands and regions.

We've invested meaningfully in stores and digital to improve the customer experience while partnerships and new product categories are helping us reach new customers. While early, it's really encouraging to see how our evolved model is driving brand love, awareness and consideration even in well-established markets like the U.S. It's also exciting to see how we can make this much progress on global brand growth, including new partnerships and categories for our brands while still balancing our other 3 business priorities. We've been able to manage inventory and control promotions by focusing on giving the customer the fashion and fit they value, which has enabled gross margin improvement despite freight cost pressure.

We've continued to invest in our digital systems and infrastructure, including deploying AI-based solutions across the customer journey, which we believe is critical to brand health and relevance. And of course, with our strong culture of financial discipline, we've been able to keep our double-digit operating margins and also repurchased 9% of shares outstanding year-to-date. Three quarters in, 2026 has been a year of consistent profitable growth. That momentum, along with a strong start to November, has led us to increase our full year sales and profitability outlook.

We now expect to deliver our fourth consecutive year of net sales growth, another year of strong double-digit operating margins and growth in net income per diluted share while maintaining our target of returning at least $500 million to shareholders through share repurchases. The consistency of our results reflects the strength of our operating model, the health of our global brands and the financial discipline of this team. To our associates around the world, thank you for everything you do to deliver for our customers every day. I'm incredibly proud of what we continue to accomplish together. We're ready for holiday and a strong finish to 2026, and we look forward to updating you all on both in early March.

And with that, I'll hand it over to Robert.

Robert J. Ball: Thanks, Fran, and good morning, everyone. I'd also like to thank our teams around the world for another strong quarter and the work they've done to position us for the holiday season. We delivered record third quarter net sales of $1.44 billion, up 11% year-over-year and above the 5% to 6% growth range we provided in August. Comparable sales increased 6% and AUR grew high single digits, supported by lower promotional activity, driving better-than-expected results to our outlook. We're particularly encouraged by the quality and balance of that growth. Both brands in all 3 regions delivered double-digit net sales growth with sequential acceleration across the business. Starting with our regions.

Net sales increased 11% in the Americas, 12% in EMEA and 24% in APAC. Comparable sales increased 5% in the Americas, 4% in EMEA and 24% in APAC. By brand, Abercrombie brands net sales increased 12% with comparable sales up 6%. Hollister delivered 11% net sales growth against last year's record with comparable sales up 5%. Both brands grew net sales across all 3 regions. Across regions and brands, the spread between net sales growth and comparable sales was driven by net new stores and third-party channel performance. Importantly, we're seeing contributions from multiple sources of growth. Our existing direct-to-consumer business continues to perform, while the new stores and third-party distribution partnerships are providing incremental opportunities to reach customers.

That combination is encouraging as we think about the durability of our growth. Turning to profitability. Third quarter operating income was $223 million, and operating margin was 15.5% of sales, above the 13% to 14% outlook we provided in August. Our results included approximately $20 million of IEEPA tariff refunds, contributing around 150 basis points to operating margin, consistent with our outlook. But importantly, the underlying business also performed ahead of our expectations. At the midpoint of our outlook, operating margin exceeded expectations by approximately 200 basis points, driven primarily by stronger gross margin performance from higher AUR with additional benefit from expense leverage. Year-over-year, operating margin increased approximately 350 basis points from 12% in the third quarter of 2025.

Of that improvement, approximately 150 basis points came from tariff refunds and approximately 80 basis points came from lower year-over-year tariff expense on products sold in the quarter. Higher Q3 AUR was mostly offset by higher freight, so the remaining approximately 100 basis points of operating margin improvement came from operating expense leverage, particularly from marketing and fulfillment and selling expense. We're encouraged by our operating margin performance. Higher AUR and disciplined promotional activity are supporting gross margins, while continued expense management is allowing us to invest in the business and maintain strong profitability. And importantly, we're delivering that performance while continuing to make investments in stores, digital capabilities and the infrastructure needed to support future growth.

Moving below operating income. Our tax rate for the quarter was 29%, better than expected, primarily due to stronger international performance. Net income per diluted share was $3.82 compared with diluted earnings per share of $2.36 last year and above our outlook of $2.90 to $3.20. Both our third quarter outlook and results include approximately $0.40 per share of benefit from IEEPA tariff refunds. We do not currently anticipate any additional IEEPA tariff refunds, and we updated the table in the press release and investor presentation with additional detail on the timing of tariff refunds and ongoing tariff expense. Turning to inventory and the balance sheet.

Ending inventory at cost increased around 4% year-over-year with units up mid-single digits and aligned with our expectations for unit sales growth. We believe inventory remains appropriately positioned as we enter the holiday season, giving our teams flexibility to respond to consumer demand while maintaining promotional discipline. On the balance sheet, we ended the quarter with $724 million of cash and cash equivalents and approximately $1.2 billion of liquidity. During the quarter, we repurchased $100 million worth of shares, bringing year-to-date repurchases to $382 million and our 11th consecutive quarter of share repurchases. Year-to-date, that represents approximately 9% of beginning of year shares outstanding. We ended the quarter with $468 million remaining on our current repurchase authorization.

Our approach to capital allocation remains consistent. We're investing in the business to support profitable growth, maintaining a strong balance sheet and returning excess cash to shareholders. Turning to the outlook. Our strong third quarter performance and expectations for higher fourth quarter demand support an increase to our full year sales and EPS outlook. As a reminder, our full year outlook includes the entire $120 million of tariff refunds recognized during the second and third quarters. We estimate that the refund will contribute approximately 220 basis points to full year operating margin and approximately $2.15 to full year diluted earnings per share.

Separate from the refunds, our outlook continues to reflect current Section 301 tariff rates of 10% to 12.5% effective on global imports into the U.S. Based on those assumptions, we continue to expect approximately 10 basis points of year-over-year gross margin favorability from lower year-over-year tariff expense on sold product, which we expect to be more than offset by higher freight costs. For the full year, we now expect net sales growth of around 6% from the $5.27 billion in 2025, with growth across regions and brands. We now expect mid-single-digit AUR improvement for the full year and approximately 20 basis points of benefit to net sales from foreign currency.

We continue to expect full year operating margin in the range of 14.5% to 15%, including approximately 220 basis points of benefit from the IEEPA tariff refunds. We're forecasting a tax rate around 28%, diluted weighted average shares of around 44 million and net income per diluted share in the range of $13.70 to $13.90. The EPS outlook includes an estimated $2.15 from IEEPA tariff refunds. For capital allocation, we expect capital expenditures around $260 million. We remain on track to deliver approximately 130 new store experiences, including 50 new stores and 80 remodels and rightsizes against approximately 20 closures.

These investments remain an important part of our growth strategy, allowing us to expand our reach while improving productivity and customer experience of our existing fleet. We continue to expect at least $500 million of share repurchases for 2026. For the fourth quarter of 2026, we expect net sales growth of around 7% from the Q4 2025 level of $1.67 billion with growth across regions and brands. We expect a fourth quarter operating margin around 15%, a modest expansion from the fourth quarter of 2025. This is largely in line with our prior outlook, but has been updated to reflect some incremental freight expense of around 100 basis points.

Despite the increase in freight, we continue to expect modest gross margin improvement year-over-year, partially offset by modest expense deleverage, mainly from incentive compensation. We expect a fourth quarter tax rate of around 28% and net income per diluted share in the range of $4.40 to $4.60. Diluted weighted average shares are expected to be around 43 million, including the anticipated impact of at least $120 million of fourth quarter share repurchases. To close, we are pleased with our third quarter results and the consistency of execution we've delivered throughout 2026. We've now delivered 16 consecutive quarters of sales growth, and we're doing it while maintaining strong profitability, investing in the business and returning significant capital to shareholders.

What gives us conviction in our model isn't just our performance in any single quarter, but it's also our consistency. We're seeing healthy, sustained growth across our core business, expanding our reach through new stores and partnerships and maintaining the financial discipline needed to support both. As we look ahead, we're focused on adding to this track record while investing in the opportunities that can drive our next chapter of growth. We believe that we're well positioned to finish 2026 strong and carry that momentum into 2027, and we look forward to covering both in early March. With that, operator, we're ready for questions.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Our first question will be coming from the line of Dana Lauren Telsey of Telsey Advisory Group.

Dana Lauren Telsey: Congratulations on the nice results. The sequential improvement in each of the brands and regions is very impressive. As you think about the cadence of the quarter, anything to do with the warm weather? Or did that only help you sell, whether it's more sweaters or jeans or other types of items? Anything you saw there? And any distinction in trends between women's and men's? And lastly, just following up on the freight costs. How much of a headwind do you see that going forward? And do your contracts or long-term agreements help to minimize the impact?

Fran Horowitz: Dana, -- so yes, wow, what a quarter. We're really proud and excited about delivering our 16th consecutive quarter, pleased across the entire quarter. The team knows that my favorite word is balance, and we sure found it this quarter. So we saw growth across brands, regions, genders, channels, really, really terrific with improved AUR and a strong bottom line. So it's just proof that the customers are responding to our value proposition. Women's and men's trends again, strong for all -- for both genders across both brands. And I'll hand it over to Robert to talk about the freight.

Robert J. Ball: Yes. Dana, so to your point, freight is a real cost pressure, and we've navigated it before. Our teams have a pretty strong playbook in place, and our operating model ultimately works. Freight did pressure gross margin in Q3 and does remain a headwind for us in Q4, but we're still expecting to grow our gross margins year-over-year in both quarters here. We're not assuming any rates reverse in 2027. We actively manage through sourcing, transportation timing, receipts through our Read & React model. That served us well over a range of different environments, and that's how we see things going forward.

Operator: And our next question will be coming from the line of Corey Tarlowe of Jefferies.

Corey Tarlowe: I guess, Fran, the Abercrombie business has been performing quite nicely. Could you talk a little bit about where you see opportunity within the assortment going forward? Is it more about men's, women's, specific categories? Curious your thoughts there as we head into the holiday.

Fran Horowitz: Corey, yes, wow, congrats to the Abercrombie team. Really excited to see that acceleration, 12% growth, really, really terrific and an acceleration from Q2. The brand is very healthy. We actually saw, as you mentioned, great performance across multiple categories. So we push for balance. We never want to focus too much on any one category. So we're seeing really strong performance in, I mean, best sweater quarter we've ever had. Bottoms are selling, strength across actually tops and bottoms, also strength across stores and digital. So really exciting to see across the world that we saw strength across the regions, double digits, as we said. Lots of exciting things happening. The team is ready to go.

I was in the mall this weekend, lots of excitement out there. We are ready to compete for the fourth quarter.

Corey Tarlowe: Great. And just, Robert, I wanted to follow up on the AUR opportunity. It seems like it nicely outperformed expectations this quarter. Could you unpack the drivers of that and what you might expect on that front as we look into holiday?

Robert J. Ball: Yes, really happy with where AUR shook out for the third quarter. I think it's a nice reflection of really strong product acceptance and really disciplined inventory management and promotions. We grew AUR high singles in Q3. We expect mid-single-digit growth now here for the full year. And I think it's really a testament to the teams and the fashion and the value that we are putting together for that consumer. We're delivering against both of those things, and it's showing up in strong demand and ultimately less discounting. For Q4, we still expect AUR growth, not really expecting any sort of major pullbacks on promos. We're happy with where the promotional cadence is for this holiday season.

But again, it starts with great products, disciplined inventory. We love the assortments that are out there for the holiday and the teams are ready to compete. So we'll see where we can go.

Operator: And our next question will be coming from the line of Marni Shapiro of The Retail Tracker.

Marni Shapiro: Congrats on a great quarter. The stores look fantastic. I'm curious if you could just give us an update on 2 things. Some of the newer stores you're opening are a little bit smaller, especially in the cities. And I'm curious what you're learning from that? And if it's possible, is it opening up more opportunities for you, specifically at Abercrombie? And then also at the Abercrombie brand, one of my favorite things out there right now is your holiday T-shirt pack. There's a bigger base of core items at Abercrombie than there was in prior years.

And I'm curious when you look at the assortment now, do you feel like there's a good solid almost replenishment mode or things that people are very reliant upon at Abercrombie, especially on the men's side?

Fran Horowitz: Marni, thank you. Let's see. We'll start with the newer stores. So we have been working on our real estate strategy for quite some time, as you're well aware. And we certainly have reduced the size of our stores over time, walking away specifically from any of the big flagships that we used to do. The opportunity that we have, which is super exciting, is to have different sized stores in different markets depending upon what that digital information is telling us when we open up those stores. We're excited about our street locations. I think you mentioned those.

So we're seeing nice responses in Newbury Street in Boston to Greenwich, Connecticut, and that's an opportunity for the Abercrombie store specifically. So yes, right size, right location, right economics, what you hear me say all the time, stores really do matter. And as far as core product goes, again, I'm going to go back to my balance comment. #2891. Stock Market Today, Nov. 24: Tech Sell-Off Drags Nasdaq as Dow Gains on Value Rotation

Investors rotated into healthcare and value names as AI momentum cooled and retailers' earnings beats lifted sentiment, today, Nov. 24, 2026.

By Howard Smith – Nov 24, 2026 at 4:51PM EST

Down 0.50% to 7,201, the S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.50%) slipped on tech weakness, while the Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -1.48%) fell 1.48% to 23,734 as artificial intelligence momentum cooled. In comparison, the Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI +0.85%) gained 0.85% to 50,019 as investors favored value sectors.

Gold fell 2.67% to $3,554.00 as of U.S. market close, the 10-Year Treasury yield held steady at 4.92%, and healthcare led sector gains. Industrials lagged, and technology dropped 1.54% relative to the broader market.

Kohl's Corp. jumped 19.48%, and Dollar Tree Inc. surged 11.18% after reporting quarterly results that topped expectations. Among specific tech names, Analog Devices Inc. tumbled 17.37%, and Hewlett Packard Enterprise Co. fell 16.31% amid the broad technology sell-off.

Artificial intelligence leaders retreated after yesterday's rally, with Nvidia (NVDA -5.86%) dropping 5.86% as traders take some profits in high-growth semiconductor names amid the broader tech sell-off today.

That came after consumer confidence data coupled with several strong earnings reports gave investors reason to shift into value and consumer-oriented names. The Conference Board's headline confidence index jumped to 103.3, up 11.4 points from October and well above the 92.2 consensus forecast.

Defensive sectors in general helped boost the Dow index. Today's moves are a good reminder for investors to stay diversified, as one sector's slide can result in another's gains.

#2889. Stock Market Today, Nov. 24: Hecla Mining Slides as Silver Prices Fall on Rate-Hike Worries

On Nov. 24, Hecla Mining shares slipped as a premarket silver selloff weighed on the miner, and investors are watching metal prices closely.

By Lawrence Rothman, CFA – Nov 24, 2026 at 4:53PM EST

Hecla Mining (HL -2.68%), a silver and gold miner, closed at $10.90, down 2.7%. Silver miners were under pressure in premarket trading after silver prices fell on rate-hike concerns, and investors are watching metal prices and upcoming management commentary.Trading volume reached 48.6M shares, coming in about 5% above its three-month average of 32.3M shares.

S&P 500 (SNPINDEX: ^GSPC) fell 0.5% to 7,201.09, while the Nasdaq Composite (NASDAQINDEX: ^IXIC) dropped 1.5% to 23,73.54. Among silver and gold mining peers, Pan American Silver (PAAS -3.87%) closed at $31.07, down 3.9%, and Coeur Mining (CDE -2.27%) finished at $11.19, down 2.3%.

Hecla Mining's stock, like other precious metal mining companies, is sensitive to commodity prices and interest rates. Naturally, higher commodity prices typically translate into increased revenue. However, interest rates also feed into commodity prices.

Typically, they move in opposite directions. That's because they become less attractive as interest rates move higher since physical commodities don't earn anything. Holding other assets, such as bonds or dividend-paying stocks, becomes more attractive in higher-interest rate environments.

Earlier this month, Hecla Mining reported third-quarter results. Sales grew more than 18% from the second quarter to $394.8 million. Management provided guidance on production and costs, but investors will likely focus on macroeconomic issues that affect commodity prices until the next quarterly earnings release.

#2886. Dell Just Reported Earnings and Raised Its Forecast by $10 Billion. Is the Stock a Buy After Tuesday's Selloff?

Dell shares fell 12% on Tuesday after a rival warned about AI-server profits. Hours later, Dell reported higher margins.

By Jonathan Ponciano – Nov 24, 2026 at 4:54PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Dell Technologies ›

Dell Technologies (DELL -11.83%) shares rose about 3% to $305 in after-hours trading Tuesday after the company reported that quarterly revenue more than doubled and raised its full-year forecast. The stock had closed at $295.28 in the regular session, down 12% from Monday's $334.91, as AI hardware shares sold off.

Hewlett Packard Enterprise (HPE -16.31%) led that decline. Its shares fell about 16% after executives said on Monday's earnings call that memory and other components could stay scarce through at least the second half of 2028. HPE also guided for its cloud and AI business to keep about 14% of sales as operating profit in fiscal 2027, down from 18.4% in the quarter it just reported. Dell, which sells many of the same servers, fell with it.

Dell's earnings release for the quarter ended Oct. 30 showed revenue of $54.9 billion, up 103% from a year earlier. And adjusted earnings rose 265% to $9.45 per share, well above the $6.59 analysts expected.

Most of the growth came from the Infrastructure Solutions Group, which sells servers, networking, and storage. Its revenue tripled to $42.0 billion. AI-optimized server sales rose 291% to $22.0 billion, and traditional servers and networking rose 235% to $15.0 billion. "We are now a different company," Vice Chairman and Chief Operating Officer Jeff Clarke said in the release.

Dell booked $60.2 billion of AI orders in the quarter and ended it with a record AI backlog of $132.3 billion, about two-thirds of the revenue it expects for the whole year.

Gross margin was 21% of revenue, up from 20.7% a year earlier. Helping to drive that increase, the infrastructure business kept 17% of its sales as operating profit, compared with 12.4% a year ago, and its operating income quadrupled to $7.1 billion.

Lower spending also helped. Operating expenses fell to 7.5% of revenue from 12.9%, and Chief Financial Officer David Kennedy said that is driving "higher profitability, even as AI servers become a larger part of our business." AI servers tend to carry thinner margins than storage, so a rising AI share usually pulls margins down, but this quarter it clearly didn't.

On the other hand, PCs were the weak spot. The Client Solutions Group grew revenue by 3% to $12.9 billion, consumer sales fell by 15%, and the segment's operating income slipped by 6% to $704 million.

Dell raised its full-year revenue forecast by $10 billion to $202 billion, up 78% from last year, and its AI server sales forecast to $78 billion from $74 billion. It now expects adjusted earnings of $29.68 per share, up from $25.50. For the fourth quarter, it guided revenue to $56.3 billion and adjusted earnings to $8.40 per share.

Separately, the company returned a record $4.9 billion to shareholders through buybacks and dividends in the quarter, and its board approved an additional $25 billion in repurchases. Free cash flow rose to $3.5 billion from $506 million a year earlier.

Inventories rose to $29.4 billion on Oct. 30 from $10.4 billion at the end of January, and total debt rose to $37.7 billion from $31.5 billion. If component costs keep climbing, as HPE expects, Dell may need to keep raising prices, and one quarter of higher margins doesn't show it can do that indefinitely.

The stock is also volatile. It rose about 20% on Monday after Nvidia (NVDA -5.86%) reinstated CEO Jensen Huang, then gave back most of that gain on Tuesday.

Even after Tuesday's after-hours gain, the stock trades at about 10 times the adjusted earnings Dell expects for the fiscal year ending in January. That's a low multiple for a company whose revenue just doubled and whose backlog covers much of next year. And while the stock fell on worries about a rival's margins, Dell's own margins rose.

The case depends on AI spending holding up, the same bet behind most AI stocks. Investors who already own plenty of semiconductor stocks and other AI infrastructure names should know Dell adds to that exposure rather than diversifying it. Those who want to see whether component costs hit margins can wait for next quarter's report.

#2885. Lazio, Nicolae racconta la crescita di Provstgaard e parla dell’Inter

Dal ritiro con il Vejle all’offerta biancoceleste: il dirigente ripercorre il lavoro svolto con il difensore danese.

Mario Nicolae ripercorre il percorso di Oliver Provstgaard, dal Vejle alla Lazio, e ritiene che il difensore potrebbe fare bene anche all’Inter, club al quale viene accostato. Secondo quanto riportato da TuttoMercatoWeb, Il dirigente, che oggi lavora al Rapid Bucarest, racconta la fiducia data al danese quando era un giovane dell’accademia e le tappe che lo hanno portato al trasferimento in biancoceleste.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Nicolae aveva già definito Provstgaard «un vero gioiello pronto al grande salto». Ora ricorda il progetto costruito attorno a lui, l’offerta della Lazio e il ruolo avuto dai suoi agenti nell’operazione.

Nicolae incontrò Provstgaard al Vejle, in Danimarca. Il dirigente ripercorre i suoi primi mesi nel club e la preparazione del ritiro: «Quando sono arrivato la squadra era ultima in classifica in Danimarca, a dicembre. Ci stavamo organizzando per il ritiro ad Antalya in Turchia, stavamo facendo la rosa di chi sarebbe venuto con noi. Ero nuovo, ho sentito tante opinioni tra proprietà e staff sui giocatori interessanti, quelli con cui proseguire. Mi hanno accennato di Oliver che in quel momento era giovane, veniva dall'accademia ma arrivava dai problemi ai crociati e non giocava da mesi».

Nonostante l’infortunio e i mesi senza giocare, le qualità del difensore convinsero Nicolae a portarlo in Turchia: «Però era giovane, di prospettiva, fisicamente molto interessante e intelligente. Mi è sembrato giusto portarlo subito in ritiro con noi. Ho iniziato a conoscerlo e ho rinnovato subito il suo contratto!»

La scelta di coinvolgerlo nel ritiro fu dunque il primo passo di un percorso che, secondo Nicolae, richiedeva fiducia e lavoro condiviso. Il dirigente aveva ascoltato le valutazioni interne prima di puntare sul giovane centrale.

Il successivo cambio di allenatore diede continuità al progetto sul difensore. Nicolae racconta: «A marzo abbiamo cambiato allenatore, abbiamo preso un tecnico croato molto giovane come Ivan Prelec, che aveva esperienza nell'accademia della Dinamo Zagabria e quindi coi ragazzi. Gli ho accennato il progetto Provstgaard, lì siamo retrocessi ma Oliver ha esordito tra i pro in Serie A. Il secondo anno siamo tornati subito su, vincendo la Serie B danese con lui titolarissimo».

Nicolae sottolinea che non si trattò soltanto di credere nelle qualità del giocatore. «Non ho solo avuto fiducia e visione su Provstgaard ma c'è stato anche un progetto con lui, con allenatore e proprietà, abbiamo detto che era un prospetto della società con cui c'era da lavorare per creare un prodotto per una grande lega europea. Gli abbiamo dato fiducia anche quando, e capitava poche volte, non giocava bene», spiega.

Al lavoro del club si aggiunse quello di Oliver. Nicolae ne ripercorre i progressi, dall’Under 21 e dalla fascia di capitano della selezione giovanile fino al posto da titolare nella massima serie danese. Ricorda anche la cessione, avvenuta nella settimana in cui il Vejle fu venduto, e la propria successiva partenza: «Ha lavorato come un pazzo: è arrivato in Under 21, poi è stato capitano dell'Under, in Serie A poi ha giocato chiaramente da titolare. Poi siamo riusciti a venderlo alla Lazio proprio nella settimana in cui il club è stato poi venduto e poi sono andato via anche io… La Lazio ha fatto la miglior offerta, c'era il Pisa con un'offerta ufficiale e anche altri club. Tramite i suoi agenti, che avevano già Isaksen alla Lazio, è stata un'operazione importante e chiaramente questo è stato un fattore». La Lazio presentò l’offerta migliore; il Pisa ne aveva formulata una ufficiale e c’erano anche altre società interessate.

Nel percorso al Vejle fu importante anche Raul Albentosa, capitano spagnolo della squadra. Nicolae collega l’ambizione di Provstgaard al sostegno ricevuto dal compagno: «Ha sempre voluto crescere, ha sempre voluto arrivare in un top club nel Mondo. Al Vejle abbiamo fatto grandi cose insieme, è stata l'esperienza perfetta per lui. E' cresciuto al fianco del nostro capitano spagnolo, Raul Albentosa, che è stato un fratello per lui. Oltre 100 presenze in Liga e lo ha guidato passo dopo passo in questo progetto di due anni e mezzo».

Per Nicolae, Midtjylland e Copenhagen avrebbero rappresentato un salto importante ma interno al calcio danese. Riteneva invece Provstgaard pronto per una grande squadra anche fuori dalla Danimarca: «Era già pronto per andare in un top club. Poteva andare al Midtjylland, al Copenhagen, ma sarebbe stato solo un salto di livello interno anche se molto importante. Dal Vejle alla Lazio è stato il grande salto, chiaramente, ma sì, era già pronto. L'Italia ha sempre apprezzato i giocatori scandinavi, non è un caso che ci abbiano pensato. Per me vederlo in prima pagina su un grande quotidiano è un grande orgoglio».
